Geometric fields, ranks, and generic derivations
Antongiulio Fornasiero, Elliot Kaplan, Angus Matthews

Abstract
. In this note, we show various minimality results for a geometric theory of fields T T : T T is stable if and only if it is strongly minimal, T T is simple if and only if it has SU-rank 1, and T T is rosy if and only if T T is surgical. Combining the first equivalence with an earlier result of Hrushovski, we deduce that algebraically bounded stable fields are precisely expansions of algebraically closed fields by constants. We then consider algebraically bounded and o-minimal expansions of fields with generic derivations. We show that if 𝕄 \mathbb{M} is a simple algebraically bounded structure and Δ \Delta is a generic tuple of derivations on 𝕄 \mathbb{M} , then ( 𝕄 ; Δ ) (\mathbb{M};\Delta) is supersimple if and only if the derivations commute. Similarly, if 𝕄 \mathbb{M} is an o-minimal structure and Δ \Delta is a generic tuple of T T -derivations on 𝕄 \mathbb{M} , then ( 𝕄 ; Δ ) (\mathbb{M};\Delta) is superrosy if and only if the derivations commute. We obtain explicit bounds on ranks using the Kolchin polynomial.



1991 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary: 03C45; Secondary: 12L12; 12H05; 03C64

Introduction

Geometric theories of fields

In many interesting fields and expansions of fields, the model-theoretic algebraic closure
\acl
has the exchange property:
a\in\acl(A,b)\setminus\acl(A)\Rightarrow b\in\acl(A,a)
.
Examples include:

(1)

All algebraically bounded fields (for example, algebraically closed and pseudofinite fields)
[
45
]
.

(2)

All o-minimal expansions of ordered fields
[
40
, Theorem 4.1]
.

(3)

All
C
-minimal expansions of valued fields
[
27
]
.

(4)

All 1-h-minimal expansions of characteristic zero valued fields.
In the equicharacteristic case, this is
[
9
, Lemma 5.3.5]
.
The mixed characteristic case follows by considering the finest equicharacteristic coarsening and applying
[
8
, Lemma 2.5.3]
.

These fields necessarily eliminate the quantifier
\exists^{\infty}
(any definable family of finite sets has a bound on the cardinalities of the sets), and so definable sets come equipped with a definable dimension.
We call a theory of fields in which
\acl
always satisfies exchange a
geometric theory of fields
.

Often, exchange for
\acl
follows from some form of minimality.
For example, if
T
is strongly minimal then
\acl
satisfies exchange in all models of
T
.
More generally, if
T
is supersimple of SU-rank 1 (or even superrosy of þ-rank 1), then
\acl
satisfies exchange.
It is folklore that superrosy theories of þ-rank 1 are precisely the
surgical theories
of Gagelman
[
23
]
; we provide a proof in Appendix
A
.
Of course, many geometric theories of fields are not rosy; any field with a nontrivial definable valuation is not rosy
[
32
, Fact 1.8]
.

In the first part of this note, we show that for theories of fields, being geometric is actually
equivalent
to minimality under appropriate assumptions.
More precisely, we show:

Theorem A

(Theorems
2.5
,
2.7
, and
2.10
)
.

Let
T
be a geometric theory of fields.

(1)

If
T
is stable, then
T
is strongly minimal.

(2)

If
T
is simple, then
T
is supersimple of SU-rank 1.

(3)

If
T
is rosy, then
T
is surgical.

We note that these minimality results do not hold for Kim-independence in NSOP
1
-theories; see Remark
2.14
.

A theory
T
is said to be
algebraically bounded
if model-theoretic algebraic closure agrees with field-theoretic algebraic closure over
\mathbb{P}\coloneqq\dcl(\emptyset)
in every model
\mathbb{M}\models T
.
That is, if

a\in\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(B)\iff\trdeg(a|\mathbb{P}(B))=0

for
a\in\mathbb{M}
and
B\subseteq\mathbb{M}
.
This is not van den Dries’ original definition of algebraic boundedness, but it is equivalent
[
26
]
.
Combining Theorem
A
with an earlier result of Hrushovski
[
24
]
, we obtain:

Corollary
2.6
.

Any algebraically bounded stable field is an expansion of an algebraically closed field by constants.

On the other hand, Hrushovski demonstrates that there are many strongly minimal expansions of an algebraically closed field (all of which are necessarily geometric)
[
24
]
.
The rigidity observed above for algebraically bounded stable fields does not hold for simple fields.
Indeed, any bounded perfect pseudo-algebraically closed (PAC) field is algebraically bounded and supersimple of SU-rank 1
[
25
]
, as is the expansion of an algebraically closed field by a generic predicate
[
7
]
.
Of course, it is conjectured that the only supersimple pure fields are bounded perfect PAC which, combined with Theorem
A
, implies that these are also the only geometric simple pure fields.
There is a similar conjecture for superrosy pure fields, where PAC is replaced by
pseudo-real closed

[
32
]
.
This can be similarly extended to geometric rosy pure fields using Theorem
A
.

Expansions by generic derivations

Beginning in Section
3
, we turn our attention to ranks on geometric fields (always of characteristic zero) equipped with a generic tuple of derivations.
As of now, there is no general framework for expanding a geometric field by derivations and obtaining a model completion (the naive approach fails in general, see
[
19
]
), though there are some frameworks in which things can be done
assuming
the existence of a model companion
[
33
,
42
]
.
While our methods can likely be generalized to these frameworks, we restrict our attention to the two settings where the expansion by a tuple of derivations is known to admit a model completion: algebraically bounded structures
[
18
]
and o-minimal structures
[
16
]
.
Note that in
[
16
]
, the model companion is only shown to exist for o-minimal structures with several
commuting
compatible derivations; though, as we show in Appendix
B
, the model companion also exists in the noncommuting case.

Let
T
be an algebraically bounded or o-minimal theory extending the theory of fields of characteristic zero.
Let
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
be the theory of models of
T
equipped with a finite tuple
\Delta
of derivations (compatible in the o-minimal case; see Definition
3.3
), and let
T^{\Delta}
extend
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
by axioms stating that the derivations in
\Delta
commute.
Both
T^{\Delta}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
have model completions, which we denote by
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
respectively.
In Corollary
3.6
, we show that the theories
T^{\Delta}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
fit into the framework of
derivation-like theories
from
[
33
]
.
We use this to deduce the following:

Theorem B

(Corollaries
3.12
,
3.13
, and
3.14
)
.

(1)

If
T
is simple, then so are
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
.

(2)

If
T
is NSOP
1
, then so are
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
.

(3)

If
T^{\Delta}_{g}
has GEI, then it is rosy; likewise for
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
.

Note that (
1
) and (
2
) hold vacuously for o-minimal theories, and that (
1
) and (
3
) were already established for algebraically bounded theories in
[
20
]
.
In the o-minimal case, (
3
) holds unconditionally, since
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
are known to eliminate imaginaries; see
[
16
, Theorem 5.19]
and Corollary
B.10
.
It is conjectured that GEI always transfers from
T
to
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}

[
20
, Conjecture 3.7]
, a result that is known to hold for certain topological theories
[
10
,
20
]
.
The results in Theorem
B
contribute to a growing list of model-theoretic properties known to transfer from
T
to
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
, including stability, NIP, and distality
[
18
,
20
,
16
]
, as well as NTP
2
and the antichain tree property in the case of a single derivation
[
28
]
.

Generic derivations and ranks

In Section
4
, we turn to an analysis of ranks in
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
.
Let
m\coloneqq|\Delta|
denote the number of derivations.
First, we investigate supersimplicity, superstability, and superrosiness, showing that these transfer in the commuting case:

Theorem C

(Proposition
4.2
, Theorem
4.9
)
.

If
m\geqslant 2
, then
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
is never superrosy (thus, never supersimple or superstable).
On the other hand:

(1)

If
T
is simple, then
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is supersimple of SU-rank
\omega^{m}
.

(2)

If
T^{\Delta}_{g}
has GEI, then
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is superrosy of þ-rank
\omega^{m}
.

The key tool in proving Theorem
C
is the Kolchin polynomial (or an appropriate modification thereof in the o-minimal case
[
17
]
).
It has long been known that the Kolchin polynomial can be used to bound ranks in differentially closed fields
[
37
]
, and we show that SU-rank and þ-rank can be bounded by the Kolchin polynomial in our setting as well.

Following this, we characterize when
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
are strongly dependent or, more broadly, strong.
Strong theories are a subclass of NTP
2
theories, and strongly dependent theories are those strong theories that are also NIP.

Theorem D

(Proposition
4.11
, Theorem
4.12
)
.

If
m>1
, then
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
is never strong.
The theory
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is strong (respectively, strongly dependent) if and only if
T
is simple (respectively, stable).

Note that in the case that
T
is stable, then Corollary
2.6
tells us that it must be an expansion of ACF
0
by constants.
Thus,
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
are expansions of DCF
0,m
and DCF
{}^{\operatorname{nc}}_{0,m}
by constants, respectively.
It follows from known results about differentially closed fields that
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is superstable of U-rank
\omega^{m}
, and thus strong
[
37
]
.

A natural question is whether the GEI assumptions in Theorems
B
(
3
) and
C
(
2
) can be dropped.
As mentioned, this would follow from
[
20
, Conjecture 3.7]
, but it would also follow from the weaker conjecture:

Conjecture
.

If
T
is rosy and algebraically bounded (thus, surgical), then
T^{\Delta}_{g}
and
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
have GEI.

1.
Independence relations

Let
T
be a complete
\mathcal{L}
-theory with infinite models, and let
\mathbb{M}\models T
be a monster model.
We will use
A
,
B
, and
C
to denote small subsets of
\mathbb{M}
.

Definition 1.1

(Adler
[
3
]
)
.

An
independence relation
on
\mathbb{M}
is a ternary relation
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
between small subsets of
\mathbb{M}
which satisfies the following axioms:

(1)

Invariance:
If
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
and
(A^{*},B^{*},C^{*})\equiv_{\mathcal{L}}(A,B,C)
, then
A^{*}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C^{*}}B^{*}
.

(2)

Monotonicity:
If
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
,
A_{0}\subseteq A
, and
B_{0}\subseteq B
, then
A_{0}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B_{0}
.

(3)

Base monotonicity:
Suppose
C_{0}\subseteq C\subseteq B
.
If
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C_{0}}B
, then
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
.

(4)

Transitivity:
Suppose
D\subseteq C\subseteq B
.
If
B\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}A
and
C\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{D}A
, then
B\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{D}A
.

(5)

Normality:
If
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
, then
AC\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
.

(6)

Finite character:
If
A_{0}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
for all finite
A_{0}\subseteq A
, then
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
.

(7)

Local character:
For each
A
, there is a cardinal
\kappa(A)
such that for all
B
, there is
C\subseteq B
of cardinality
|C|<\kappa(A)
with
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
.

(8)

Full existence:
For all
A,B,C
, there is
A^{*}\equiv_{\mathcal{L}(C)}A
with
A^{*}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
.

(9)

Symmetry:
If
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
, then
B\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}A
.

An independence relation
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is
real-strict
if it satisfies:

(10)

Anti-reflexivity:
For all
a\in\mathbb{M}
, if
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{B}a
, then
a\in\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(B)
.

A
real-canonical independence relation
is a real-strict independence relation which also satisfies:

(11)

Intersection:
for
C_{1},C_{2}\subseteq B
and
D\coloneqq\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(C_{1})\cap\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(C_{2})
, we have

\textstyle A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C_{1}}B\text{ and }A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C_{2}}B\implies A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{D}B.

An independence relation is said to be
strict
if it extends to an independence relation on
\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
satisfying anti-reflexivity (where
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}
is replaced with
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}
).
Likewise, a strict independence relation is
canonical
if its extension to
\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
satisfies intersection.

Remark 1.2
.

In Adler’s definition, full existence and symmetry are replaced by

(1)

Extension:
If
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
and
B^{*}\supseteq B
, then there is
A^{*}\equiv_{\mathcal{L}(BC)}A
with
A^{*}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B^{*}
.

By
[
3
, Remark 1.2 and Theorem 2.5]
, full existence and symmetry are equivalent to extension, modulo the other axioms.
By
[
12
, Proposition 3.1.3]
, the axioms also imply

(1)

Strong closure:

A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B\iff\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(AC)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(C)}\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(BC)
.

Remark 1.3
.

If
T
has geometric elimination of imaginaries (GEI), then
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is real-strict if and only if it is strict, and
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is real-canonical if and only if it is canonical.

Given independence relations
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
and
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
on
\mathbb{M}
, we say that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is coarser than
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
if

\textstyle A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B\implies A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B

for all
A,B,C
.

Following Adler
[
3
]
, we say that
T
is
rosy
if
\mathbb{M}
admits a strict independence relation.
By
[
3
, Proposition 4.7]
, this agrees with Onshuus’ original definition of rosiness; see
[
38
,
14
]
.

Fact 1.4

(
[
3
, Theorem 4.3]
,
[
1
, Theorem 3.3]
)
.

If
\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
admits a strict independence relation
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
, then it admits a coarsest strict independence relation
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
called
þ-forking independence
.
If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is canonical, then it coincides with
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
.

Remark 1.5
.

Adler
[
3
]
defines a theory to be
real rosy
if
\mathbb{M}
admits a real-strict independence relation.
If
T
is real rosy, then
\mathbb{M}
admits a coarsest real-strict independence relation, called
real þ-forking independence
.
Note that this need not coincide with the restriction of þ-forking independence to real sets, even in rosy theories.
As we will see in Lemma
1.12
and Proposition
3.11
below, we can describe real þ-forking independence quite explicitly in the theories we consider.

Remark 1.6
.

Following the proof of
[
1
, Lemma 3.2]
, we see that if
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is a real-canonical independence relation on
\mathbb{M}
, then it is the coarsest real-strict independence relation on
\mathbb{M}
, and so coincides with real þ-forking.

Let
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
denote forking independence in
T
.

Fact 1.7

(
[
3
, Theorem 5.3]
)
.

T
is simple if and only if
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is an independence relation on
\mathbb{M}
.
If
T
is simple, then any independence relation on
\mathbb{M}
is coarser than
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
.

1.1.
Ranks

Let
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
be an independence relation on
\mathbb{M}
and let
p
be an

\mathcal{L}(A)
-type.
An
\mathcal{L}(B)
-type
q
is an
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
-forking extension of
p
if
B\supseteq A
,
q
extends
p
, and
{\bar{a}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\not$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\not$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\not$\kern 6.34859pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\kern 5.54167pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{A}B
for some (equivalently,
any) tuple
{\bar{a}}
realizing
q
.
The
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
-rank of
p
, denoted
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)
, is defined as follows:

(1)

We always have
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)\geqslant 0
, as
p
is assumed to be consistent;

(2)

For
\alpha
any ordinal,
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)\geqslant\alpha+1
if there is an
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
-forking extension
q
of
p
with
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(q)\geqslant\alpha
;

(3)

For
\lambda
any nonzero limit ordinal,
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)\geqslant\lambda
if
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)\geqslant\alpha
for all ordinals
\alpha<\lambda
.

If there is an ordinal
\alpha
with
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)\geqslant\alpha
and
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)\not\geqslant\alpha+1
, then we put
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)\coloneqq\alpha
.
Otherwise, we put
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)\coloneqq\infty
.
We say that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is
ranked
if
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)<\infty
for all types
p
.
Note that if
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is real-strict and
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)=0
, then
p
is algebraic.

For
{\bar{a}}\in\mathbb{M}^{n}
, we put
U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}({\bar{a}}|A)\coloneqq U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|A))
.
For an
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable set
X
, we set

U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(X)\coloneqq\sup\{U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}({\bar{a}}|A):{\bar{a}}\in X\}.

We also set

U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(T)\coloneqq\sup\{U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p):p\text{ is a unary $\mathcal{L}(\emptyset)$-type}\}.

Following the proof of
[
46
, Theorem 5.1.6]
, one can establish the Lascar inequalities for the
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
-rank (no changes to the proof are needed).

Fact 1.8

(Lascar inequalities)
.

For
a,b\in\mathbb{M}
, we have

U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(a|A)+U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(b|A\cup\{a\})\leqslant U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(a,b|A)\leqslant U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(a|A)\oplus U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(b|A\cup\{a\}),

where
+
is the ordinal sum and
\oplus
is the commutative (Hessenberg) sum, it being understood that both sums take value
\infty
if some summand is
\infty
.

Corollary 1.9
.

The following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is ranked;

(2)

U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(p)<\infty
for all unary types
p
;

(3)

U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}\!}(T)<\infty
.

Fact 1.10
.

Suppose
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
and
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
are independence relations on
\mathbb{M}
and that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is coarser than
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
.
Then for any type
p
, we have

U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}}(p)\geqslant U^{\tiny\!\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}{\kern 2.00034pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 1.35623pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.00034pt}{\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}}(p).

In particular, if
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{1}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is ranked, then so is
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{2}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
.

In many cases of interest, we have specific names for the
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
-rank:

(1)

If
T
is simple, then
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
-rank is called
SU-rank
, and the SU-rank of
p
is denoted
SU(p)
.
If in addition
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is ranked, then
T
is said to be
supersimple
.

(2)

If
T
is stable, then SU-rank agrees with Lascar’s U-rank.
If
T
is also supersimple, then it is said to be
superstable
.

(3)

If
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is an independence relation, then
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
-rank is called
þ-rank
, and the þ-rank of
p
is denoted
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}(p)
.
If
T
is rosy and
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
on
\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
is ranked, then
T
is said to be
superrosy
.

1.2.
Independence in pregeometric theories

Recall that
T
is
pregeometric
if
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}
is a pregeometry in any model of
T
, and that
T
is
geometric
if it is pregeometric and eliminates
\exists^{\infty}
.
Suppose that
T
is pregeometric and let
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}
denote the rank function corresponding to
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}
.
We define the
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}
-independence relation

\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
as follows:

\textstyle A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B\iff\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(A_{0}|BC)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(A_{0}|C)\text{ for every finite subset }A_{0}\subseteq A.

The following fact is well-known; we prove a stronger version in Proposition
A.3
below:

Fact 1.11
.

Suppose that
T
is pregeometric.
Then
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
is a real-strict independence relation on
\mathbb{M}
, and the
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
-rank of
\tp({\bar{a}}|B)
coincides with
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)
for all tuples
{\bar{a}}\in\mathbb{M}^{n}
.
In particular,
T
has
