AD\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
;

(2)

Any
\mathcal{L}(BC)
-definable set
X\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
containing
{\bar{a}}
also contains
U
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
3.9
, we inductively find a tuple
{\bar{d}}=(d_{1},\ldots,d_{2n})\in\ker(\Delta)^{2n}
with
A{\bar{d}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
such that

d_{2i-1}\models p_{a_{i}}^{-}|_{ABC\{d_{j}:j<2i-1\}},\qquad d_{2i}\models p_{a_{i}}^{+}|_{ABC\{d_{j}:j<2i\}}

Let
U
be the set
\prod_{i=1}^{n}(d_{2i-1},d_{2i})\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
.
Then
U
is an infinitesimal (relative to
ABC
) neighborhood of
{\bar{a}}
, so any
\mathcal{L}(BC)
-definable set
X\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
containing
{\bar{a}}
also contains
U
.
∎

Proposition 3.11
.

\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\,
is the coarsest real-strict independence relation on
(\mathbb{M};\Delta)
, and so coincides with real þ-forking independence.

Note that in case (
I
), this follows from Corollary
3.7
and Remark
1.6
.
We give here another proof that doesn’t use real-canonicity and unifies both cases.

Proof.

Let
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
be any real-strict independence relation on
(\mathbb{M};\Delta)
.
By finite character and the definition of
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\,
, it suffices to prove that for any small sets
A,B,C
with
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
and any finite tuple
{\bar{a}}
from
\Jet(A)
, we have
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|\Jet(BC))=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|\Jet(C))
.
We prove this by induction on
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|\Jet(BC))
.
Note that if
{\bar{a}}\in\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet(BC))=\acl_{\Delta}(BC)
, then strong closure and real-strictness give
{\bar{a}}\in\acl_{\Delta}(C)
, so
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|\Jet(BC))=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|\Jet(C))=0
.
We let
n>0
and assume that our induction hypothesis holds for all small sets
A,B,C
with
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
and all tuples
{\bar{a}}
from
\Jet(A)
, so long as
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|\Jet(BC))<n
.

Let
A,B,C
be small sets with
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
and let
{\bar{a}}
be a tuple from
\Jet(A)
with
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|\Jet(BC))=n
.
We need to show that
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|\Jet(C))=n
as well.
By strong closure and Corollary
3.8
, we may assume that
A,B,C
are all
\acl_{\Delta}
-closed and that
C\subseteq A,B
, so
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)=n
.
Using full existence, take an
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}(A)
-automorphism
\sigma
of
(\mathbb{M};\Delta)
with
\sigma(B)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{A}B
.
Since
C\subseteq A
, invariance and symmetry gives
\sigma(B)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}A
, and transitivity and normality give
\sigma(B)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}AB
.
Applying base monotonicity, monotonicity, and normality gives
B\sigma(B)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{B}A
.
By symmetry and our induction hypothesis, we must have

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B\sigma(B))=n.

(3.1)

We also record here that applying monotonicity to
\sigma(B)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}AB
gives

\textstyle\sigma(B)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B.

(3.2)

We now consider the algebraically bounded and o-minimal cases separately.

Case (
I
):

Let
V\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
be the Zariski locus of
{\bar{a}}
over
B
, so
V
is absolutely irreducible over
B
of dimension
n
.
Using that dimension and absolute irreducibility of Zariski-closed sets are definable in families
[
22
, Appendix]
, together with elimination of imaginaries in the theory of algebraically closed fields, we find a
\mathcal{L}(C)
-definable family
(V_{{\bar{z}}})_{{\bar{z}}\in Z}
of pairwise distinct absolutely irreducible varieties, all of the same dimension, and a tuple
{\bar{b}}\in Z
with coordinates from
B
with
V_{{\bar{b}}}=V
.
Note that
{\bar{a}}\in V_{\sigma({\bar{b}})}
as well, so
V_{{\bar{b}}}\cap V_{\sigma({\bar{b}})}
has dimension
n
by (
3.1
).
As the varieties in the family
(V_{{\bar{z}}})_{{\bar{z}}\in Z}
are pairwise distinct and absolutely irreducible, we have
{\bar{b}}=\sigma({\bar{b}})
.
By (
3.2
) and real-strictness, we have
{\bar{b}}\in\acl_{\Delta}(C)=C
, so
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|C)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|C{\bar{b}})=n
, as desired.

Case (
II
):

Let
{\bar{a}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{M}^{n}
be a subtuple of
{\bar{a}}
that is
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}
-independent over
B
.
Then
{\bar{a}}^{\prime}
is
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}
-independent over
B\sigma(B)
as well by (
3.1
).
Since
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
and
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{B}B\sigma(B)
, transitivity gives
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B\sigma(B)
.
Applying Corollary
3.10
to the sets
A,B\sigma(B),C
and the tuple
{\bar{a}}^{\prime}
, we find a finite set
D\subseteq\ker(\Delta)
and an
\mathcal{L}(D)
-definable open set
U\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
such that

(1)

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(D|AB)=|D|
and
AD\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
;

(2)

Any
\mathcal{L}(B\sigma(B))
-definable set
X\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
containing
{\bar{a}}^{\prime}
also contains
U
.

As algebraic and definable closure coincide in the o-minimal case, we may write
{\bar{a}}=f({\bar{a}}^{\prime})
for some
\mathcal{L}(B)
-definable function
f
.
Take an
\mathcal{L}(C)
-definable family
(f_{{\bar{z}}})_{{\bar{z}}\in Z}
of functions, along with a tuple
{\bar{b}}\in Z
with coordinates in
B
such that
f=f_{{\bar{b}}}
.
Consider the relation
\sim
on
Z
given by

{\bar{z}}\sim{\bar{z}}^{\prime}\iff f_{{\bar{z}}}|_{U}=f_{{\bar{z}}^{\prime}}|_{U}.

Then
\sim
is
\mathcal{L}(CD)
-definable, so definable choice yields an
\mathcal{L}(CD)
-definable map
g\colon Z\to Z
such that
{\bar{z}}\sim g({\bar{z}})
and
{\bar{z}}\sim{\bar{z}}^{\prime}\iff g({\bar{z}})=g({\bar{z}}^{\prime})
for
{\bar{z}},{\bar{z}}^{\prime}\in Z
.
The tuple
{\bar{a}}^{\prime}
is contained in the
\mathcal{L}(B\sigma(B))
-definable set

\{{\bar{x}}\in\mathbb{M}^{n}:f_{{\bar{b}}}({\bar{x}})=f_{\sigma({\bar{b}})}({\bar{x}})\},

so
U
is contained in this set as well, giving
{\bar{b}}\sim\sigma({\bar{b}})
.
Let
{\bar{b}}^{\prime}\coloneqq g({\bar{b}})=g(\sigma({\bar{b}}))
, so
f_{{\bar{b}}^{\prime}}({\bar{a}}^{\prime})={\bar{a}}
.

Now starting with
AD\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B\sigma(B)
and using base monotonicity, monotonicity, and normality we get
\sigma(B)D\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{\sigma(B)}B
.
Using (
3.2
) and applying transitivity yields
\sigma(B)D\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
.
Base monotonicity and normality give
\sigma(B)D\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{CD}BD
.
We have
{\bar{b}}^{\prime}\in\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(BD)\cap\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(\sigma(B)D)
, so
{\bar{b}}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{$$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{CD}{\bar{b}}^{\prime}
by normality.
Real-strictness gives
{\bar{b}}^{\prime}\in\acl_{\Delta}(CD)=\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(CD)
, since
\Jet(D)=D\cup\{0\}
.
Using that
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(D|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(D|B{\bar{a}})=|D|
, routine rank calculations give
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|BD)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)=n
.
Likewise,
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|CD)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|C)
.
Now we compute

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|C)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|CD)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|CD{\bar{b}}^{\prime})=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|BD)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)=n.\qed

Combining Remark
1.3
, Fact
1.4
, and Proposition
3.11
, we have:

Corollary 3.12
.

Suppose that
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
has GEI.
Then
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
is rosy and
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\,
coincides with þ-forking independence in
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
.

Note that the hypothesis of GEI is always satisfied in case (
II
) by
[
16
, Theorem 5.19]
and Corollary
B.10
.
For a different proof of Corollary
3.12
in case (
I
), see
[
20
, Corollary 6.2]
.
Combining
[
33
, Theorem 3.14]
with Corollary
2.8
, we also obtain another proof of
[
20
, Theorem 7.1]
:

Corollary 3.13
.

If
T
is simple, then so is
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
and
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\,
coincides with forking independence in
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
.

The above corollary is, of course, vacuous in case (
II
), as is the next corollary.

Corollary 3.14
.

If
T
is NSOP
1
, then so is
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
.
Moreover,
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\,
is coarser than Kim-independence in
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
.

Proof.

Suppose
T
is NSOP
1
, so we are necessarily in case (
I
).
Then in the terminology of
[
33
]
, we have that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
implies
\mathcal{L}
-compositums, since
\mathcal{L}
extends
\mathcal{L}_{\ring}
by only relations and constant symbols, and that
T
is very
\mathcal{L}
-slim (which is the same as algebraic boundedness).
The corollary follows by
[
33
, Corollary 3.19]
.
∎

Finally, we note that using Theorem
2.7
and Corollary
3.2
, we can improve
[
20
, Theorems 7.5 and 7.9]
.

Corollary 3.15
.

If
T
is simple, then
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
has GEI and eliminates imaginaries in the sorts of
T^{\eq}
.

4.
Ranks

We denote the
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
-rank by
U^{\Delta}
.
We are interested in when
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\,
is ranked.
We start by showing that we only need to consider the commuting case.
To do this, we need the following fact about
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
(\mathbb{M};\Delta)\models T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
, let
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathbb{M}
, and let
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{n}
be pairwise incomparable elements of the free monoid generated by
\Delta
(meaning that if
i\neq j
, then there is no
\gamma
for which
\gamma\theta_{i}=\theta_{j}
).
Then, viewing each
\theta_{i}
as an operator
\mathbb{M}\to\mathbb{M}
, there is
y\in\mathbb{M}
such that
\theta_{i}y=a_{i}
for each
i
.

Proof.

Immediate from the
Deep
axioms; see
[
18
, Section 4]
in case (
I
) and the appendix in case (
II
).
∎

Proposition 4.2
.

Suppose that
|\Delta|\geqslant 2
.
Then
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
is never supersimple or, more generally, superrosy.
Moreover, the independence relation
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\,
is not ranked.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon\neq\delta\in\Delta
.
For each
n\geqslant 0
, we define

C_{n}\coloneqq\bigcap_{i\leqslant n}\ker(\varepsilon\delta^{i})

Then
C_{0}\supseteq C_{1}\supseteq C_{2}\supseteq\cdots
is a descending chain of additive subgroups.
For a given
n
and
a,b\in C_{n}
, we have
a+C_{n+1}=b+C_{n+1}
if and only if
\varepsilon\delta^{n+1}a=\varepsilon\delta^{n+1}b
.
For any
d\in\mathbb{M}
, Lemma
4.1
gives
y\in\mathbb{M}
with
\varepsilon\delta^{i}y=0
for all
i\leqslant n
and
\varepsilon\delta^{n+1}y=d
.
Thus,
C_{n+1}
has infinite index in
C_{n}
.
It follows that
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
is not superrosy (in particular, not supersimple); see
[
13
, Proposition 1.4]
.
Let us show explicitly that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\,
is not ranked.
Let
d_{0}\coloneqq 0
and, assuming we have defined
d_{n}
, take
d_{n+1}
such that

(i)

\varepsilon\delta^{i}(d_{n+1}-d_{n})=0
for all
i\leqslant n
, and

(ii)

\varepsilon\delta^{n+1}(d_{n+1})\not\in\acl_{\Delta}(d_{0},\ldots,d_{n})
.

Again using Lemma
4.1
, we can always find such an element.
Note that
d_{n+1}\in\bigcap_{i\leqslant n}d_{i}+C_{i}
for each
n
.
Let
p
be a complete type over
\{d_{i}:i<\omega\}
concentrating on
\bigcap_{i<\omega}(d_{i}+C_{i})
, and for
n\geqslant 0
, let
p_{n}\coloneqq p|_{\{d_{0},\ldots,d_{n}\}}
.
Fix
n
and let
a\models p_{n+1}
.
Then
a\in d_{n+1}+C_{n+1}
, so

\varepsilon\delta^{n+1}a=\varepsilon\delta^{n+1}d_{n+1}\not\in\acl_{\Delta}(d_{0},\ldots,d_{n})=\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet(d_{0},\ldots,d_{n})),

It follows that

\textstyle\Jet(a)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\not$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\not$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\not$\kern 6.34859pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\kern 5.54167pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{\Jet(d_{0},\ldots,d_{n})}\Jet(d_{0},\ldots,d_{n+1}).

so
p_{n+1}
is an
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
-forking extension of
p_{n}
.
Thus,
U^{\Delta}(p_{0})=\infty
.
∎

4.1.
Bounding ranks via the Kolchin polynomial

For the remainder of the section,
(\mathbb{M};\Delta)
is a monster model of
T^{\Delta}_{g}
.
We let
\preccurlyeq
be the componentwise partial order on
\mathbb{N}^{m}
.
For
{\bar{u}}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
, we put

|{\bar{u}}|\coloneqq u_{1}+\cdots+u_{m},\qquad\delta^{{\bar{u}}}\coloneqq\delta_{1}^{u_{1}}\cdots\delta_{m}^{u_{m}}\colon\mathbb{M}\to\mathbb{M}.

For
{\bar{a}}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in\mathbb{M}^{d}
,
{\bar{u}}\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
, and
t\in\mathbb{N}
, we set

\delta^{\bar{u}}({\bar{a}})\coloneqq(\delta^{{\bar{u}}}a_{1},\ldots,\delta^{{\bar{u}}}a_{d}),\qquad\Jet_{\leqslant t}({\bar{a}})\coloneqq\{\delta^{{\bar{u}}}{\bar{a}}:|{\bar{u}}|\leqslant t\},\qquad\Jet({\bar{a}})\coloneqq\{\delta^{{\bar{u}}}{\bar{a}}:{\bar{u}}\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\}.

Then
\Jet({\bar{a}})
agrees with our previous definition for singletons.

Fact 4.3

(
[
17
, Corollary A]
)
.

Given
{\bar{a}}\in\mathbb{M}^{d}
and a small subset
A\subseteq M
, there is a numerical polynomial
\omega_{{\bar{a}}|A}
, called the
Kolchin polynomial
of
{\bar{a}}
over
A
, such that

\omega_{{\bar{a}}|A}(t)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet_{\leqslant t}({\bar{a}})|\Jet(A))\quad\text{for $t\gg 0$}.

Above,
t\gg 0
abbreviates “for all sufficiently large
t
.” In case (
I
),
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}
coincides with the transcendence degree so the Kolchin polynomial is the classical Kolchin polynomial from
[
29
]
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be the set of all Kolchin polynomials, totally ordered by dominance, so

P_{1}<_{e}P_{2}\iff P_{1}(t)<P_{2}(t)\quad\text{for $t\gg 0$.}

By
[
17
, Corollary 4.7]
,
\mathcal{P}
coincides with the set of Hilbert polynomials of finitely generated graded modules over a polynomial ring, which allows us to equip
\mathcal{P}
with an ordinal-valued ranking:

Fact 4.4

(
[
4
, Corollaries 3.2 and 3.15]
)
.

Each
P\in\mathcal{P}
can be written uniquely as a sum

P(T)=\binom{T+a_{1}}{a_{1}}+\binom{T+a_{2}-1}{a_{2}}+\cdots+\binom{T+a_{n}-(n-1)}{a_{n}}

for
n\geqslant 0
and
a_{1}\geqslant a_{2}\geqslant\cdots\geqslant a_{n}\geqslant 0
.
We have a strictly increasing map
\rk_{\mathcal{P}}\colon\mathcal{P}\to\mathbf{On}
(the class of ordinals) where, for
P
as above,

\rk_{\mathcal{P}}(P)\coloneqq\sum_{i\leqslant n}\omega^{a_{i}}\in\mathbf{On}\quad\text{(ordinal sum)}.

Corollary 4.5
.

Each nonzero
P\in\mathcal{P}
may be written

P(T)=\frac{n}{k!}T^{k}+\text{ lower-degree terms}.

for some nonzero
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
.
For such a
P
, we have
\omega^{k}n\leqslant\rk_{\mathcal{P}}(P)<\omega^{k}(n+1)
.

Let
p
be an
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}(A)
-type.
We set
\omega_{p}\coloneqq\omega_{{\bar{a}}|A}
, where
{\bar{a}}
is some (any) realization of
p
.
Given a small set
B\supseteq A
, we always have
\omega_{{\bar{a}}|B}\leqslant_{e}\omega_{{\bar{a}}|A}
.
It follows that
\omega_{q}\leqslant_{e}\omega_{p}
for any
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}(B)
-type
q
extending
p
.
Whether this inequality is strict is equivalent to
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
-forking:

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
A\subseteq B
, let
p
be an
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}(A)
-type, and let
q
be an
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}(B)
-type extending
p
.
Then
q
is an
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
-forking extension of
p
if and only if
\omega_{q}<_{e}\omega_{p}
.

Proof.

Let
{\bar{a}}\models q
.
If
{\bar{a}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{A}B
, then
\Jet({\bar{a}})\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{\Jet(A)}\Jet(B)
, so
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(A_{0}|\Jet(B))=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(A_{0}|\Jet(A))
for all finite
A_{0}\subseteq\Jet({\bar{a}})
.
In particular,
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet_{\leqslant t}({\bar{a}})|\Jet(B))=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet_{\leqslant t}({\bar{a}})|\Jet(A))
for all
t
, so
\omega_{{\bar{a}}|B}=\omega_{{\bar{a}}|A}
.

Conversely, suppose
{\bar{a}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\not$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\not$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\not$\kern 6.34859pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\kern 5.54167pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{A}B
and take a finite
A_{0}\subseteq\Jet({\bar{a}})
with
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(A_{0}|\Jet(B))<\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(A_{0}|\Jet(A))
.
For
t\gg 0
, we have
A_{0}\subseteq\Jet_{\leqslant t}({\bar{a}})
, so
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet_{\leqslant t}({\bar{a}})|\Jet(B))<\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet_{\leqslant t}({\bar{a}})|\Jet(A))
.
Thus
\omega_{{\bar{a}}|B}<_{e}\omega_{{\bar{a}}|A}
.
∎

Corollary 4.7
.

For any
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}(A)
-type
p
, we have
U^{\Delta}(p)\leqslant\rk_{\mathcal{P}}(\omega_{p})
.
In particular,
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\,
is ranked.

Combining this with Corollaries
3.12
and
3.13
, we get bounds on þ-rank when
T^{\Delta}_{g}
has GEI and on SU-rank when
T
is simple.

To give more a more precise computation of
U^{\Delta}
, we make use of the
\Delta
-closure operator
on
(\mathbb{M};\Delta)
, denoted by
\cl^{\Delta}
and defined by

a\in\cl^{\Delta}(A)\iff\text{$\Jet(a)$ is not $\acl_{\mathcal{L}}$-independent over $\Jet(A)$.}

Fact 4.8

(
[
17
, Theorem B]
)
.

(\mathbb{M},\cl^{\Delta})
is a pregeometry, and we have

\omega_{{\bar{a}}|A}(T)=\frac{\rk^{\Delta}({\bar{a}}|A)}{m!}T^{m}+\text{ lower-degree terms},

where
\rk^{\Delta}
is the rank function corresponding to the closure operator
\cl^{\Delta}
.
If
{\bar{a}}
is
\cl^{\Delta}
-independent over
A
, then
\omega_{{\bar{a}}|A}(T)=\binom{T+m}{m}
.

Theorem 4.9
.

We have
U^{\Delta}(T^{\Delta}_{g})=\omega^{m}
.
Moreover:

(1)

If
T
is stable, then
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is an expansion of DCF
0,m
by constants.

(2)

If
T
is simple, then
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is supersimple with
SU(T^{\Delta}_{g})=\omega^{m}
.

(3)

If
T^{\Delta}_{g}
has GEI, then
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is superrosy with
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}(T^{\Delta}_{g})=\omega^{m}
.

Proof.

Let
a\in\mathbb{M}
.
Using Facts
4.4
and
4.8
, we see that
\rk_{\mathcal{P}}(\omega_{a|\emptyset})\leqslant\omega^{m}
, with equality if and only if
a\not\in\cl^{\Delta}(\emptyset)
.
By Corollary
4.7
, this gives us an upper bound
U^{\Delta}(T^{\Delta}_{g})\leqslant\omega^{m}
.
We now show that this is an equality.
For an ordinal
\eta<\omega^{m}
, take the unique tuple
{\bar{r}}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
such that
\eta
has Cantor normal form
\sum_{i<m}\omega^{i}r_{i+1}
, and let
\delta^{\eta}
denote the operator
\delta^{\bar{r}}
.
Using saturation and the axioms of
T^{\Delta}_{g}
, we find
a\in\mathbb{M}\setminus\cl^{\Delta}(\emptyset)
.
For
\eta<\omega^{m}
, set

A_{\eta}\coloneqq\{\delta^{\mu}a:\mu\geqslant\eta\},\qquad p_{\eta}\coloneqq\tp_{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}}(a|A_{\eta}).

Then
A_{\eta}=\Jet(A_{\eta})
and each
A_{\eta}
is
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}
-independent.
We claim that
U^{\Delta}(p_{\eta})\geqslant\eta
for each
\eta
.
This is clear for
\eta=0
and follows by induction for
\eta
a limit, so it suffices to show that
p_{\eta}
is a
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
-forking extension of
p_{\eta+1}
.
Fix
\eta
and let
b\models p_{\eta}
, so

\delta^{\eta}b=\delta^{\eta}a\in A_{\eta}\setminus\acl_{\mathcal{L}}(A_{\eta+1}).

It follows that
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\not$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\not$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\not$\kern 6.34859pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\kern 5.54167pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\,\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{A_{\eta+1}}A_{\eta}
, as desired.

To see (1), suppose
T
is stable.
Then
T
expands ACF
0
by constants by Corollary
2.6
.
By uniqueness of model completions,
T^{\Delta}_{g}
must expand DCF
0,m
, the theory of differentially closed fields with
m
commuting derivations, by the same constants.
(2) and (3) are immediate by Corollaries
3.12
and
3.13
.
∎

4.2.
Strongness

An
inp-pattern of depth
\kappa
is a collection of formulas
(\varphi_{\lambda}(x,{\bar{a}}_{\lambda,i}))_{\lambda<\kappa,i<\omega}
(where
x
is unary) such that:

(1)

For
\lambda<\kappa
, the collection
\{\varphi_{\lambda}(x,{\bar{a}}_{\lambda,i}):i<\omega\}
is
k_{\lambda}
-inconsistent for some positive integer
k_{\lambda}
.

(2)

The partial type
\{\varphi_{\lambda}(x,{\bar{a}}_{\lambda,\eta(\lambda)}):\lambda<\kappa\}
is consistent for all
\eta\colon\kappa\to\omega
.

A theory has
burden
<\kappa
if there is no inp-pattern of depth
\kappa
.
A
strong theory
is a theory of burden
<\omega
.
A theory that is strong and NIP is said to be
strongly dependent
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Suppose we have an
\mathcal{L}
-formula
\varphi(x,{\bar{y}})
with
x
unary, a sequence
({\bar{b}}_{i})_{i<\omega}
, and a positive integer
k
such that
\varphi(x,{\bar{b}}_{i})
is nonalgebraic for each
i
and
\{\varphi(x,{\bar{b}}_{i}):i<\omega\}
is
k
-inconsistent.
Then
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
is not strong.

Proof.

Fix
\delta\in\Delta
and for each
\lambda<\omega
, set
\varphi_{\lambda}(x,{\bar{y}})\coloneqq\varphi(\delta^{\lambda}x,{\bar{y}})
.
One easily verifies that
(\varphi_{\lambda}(x,{\bar{b}}_{i}))_{\lambda,i<\omega}
is an inp-pattern in
T^{\Delta,?}_{g}
.
∎

Proposition 4.11
.

If
|\Delta|\geqslant 2
, then
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
is not strong.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon\neq\delta\in\Delta
and let
C\coloneqq\ker(\varepsilon)
.
Then
C
is an infinite index subgroup of the additive group
(\mathbb{M},+)
, so take
(b_{i})_{i<\omega}
in distinct cosets of
C
.
Set

\varphi_{k}(x,b_{i})\coloneqq\varepsilon(\delta^{k}x-b_{i})=0,

so
(\mathbb{M};\Delta)\models\varphi_{k}(a,b_{i})
if and only if
\delta^{k}a\in b_{i}+C
.
It follows from Lemma
4.1
that
(\varphi_{k}(x,b_{i}))_{k,i<\omega}
is an inp-pattern in
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
.
∎

Theorem 4.12
.

T^{\Delta}_{g}
is strong (resp. strongly dependent) if and only if
T
is simple (resp. stable).
Consequently,
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is strong if and only if it expands DCF
0
by constants.

Proof.

If
T
is not simple, then some formula
\varphi(x,{\bar{y}})
with
x
unary has the tree property
[
44
, Exercise 7.2.8]
.
Taking a tree of parameters
({\bar{a}}_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{<\omega}}
, we see that the collection
\{\varphi(x,{\bar{a}}_{\langle i\rangle}):i<\omega\}
is
k
-inconsistent for some
k
.
Thus,
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is not strong by Lemma
4.10
.
Conversely, if
T
is simple, then
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is supersimple by Theorem
4.9
.
Supersimple implies finite weight, which in turn implies that
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is strong
[
2
,
46
]
.

To see that
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is strongly dependent if and only if
T
is stable, use that
T^{\Delta}_{g}
is NIP if and only if
T
is; see
[
18
, Theorem 6.1]
for case (
I
) and
[
16
, Corollary 4.16]
for case (
II
).
Then these are in turn equivalent to
T^{\Delta}_{g}
expanding DCF
0
by constants by Theorem
4.9
.
∎

5.
Final remarks

Remark 5.1
.

The inequality
U^{\Delta}(p)\leqslant\rk_{\mathcal{P}}(\omega_{p})
in Corollary
4.7
cannot, in general, be improved to an equality.
Indeed, given
n>0
, Suer gives an example in the theory of differentially closed fields with two commuting derivations of a type
p
with
\rk_{\mathcal{P}}(\omega_{p})\geqslant\omega n
but with
SU(p)=U^{\Delta}(p)=\omega

[
43
, Proposition 3.45]
.

Here is another example, following
[
36
]
.
Suppose we are in case (
I
) and that
\Delta
consists of a single derivation
\delta
.
Take
a\in\mathbb{M}
with

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(a,\delta a|\emptyset)=2,\qquad\delta^{2}a=\delta a/a.

Let
p
be the type of
a
over
\emptyset
, so
\omega_{p}=2
.
Let
q
be a
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
-forking extension of
p
over a small differential subfield
K
, so
\omega_{q}\leqslant 1
.
We claim that
q
is algebraic (that is, that
\omega_{q}=0
).
Suppose that this is not the case.
Let
b\models q
and let
P(X)\in K\{X\}
be the minimal differential polynomial of
b
over
K
.
Then
P
has order 1, since
q
is nonalgebraic, and
XX^{\prime\prime}-X^{\prime}
belongs to the differential ideal
I(P)\subseteq K\{X\}
.
By
[
36
, Lemma 5.12]
,
X^{\prime}
belongs to
I(P)
as well, so
b^{\prime}=0
, contradicting the fact that no realization of
p
is constant.

A potential advantage of working with derivations on o-minimal theories is that transcendental definable functions can sometimes be used to realize these “missing” intermediate Kolchin polynomials.
Indeed, suppose we are in case (
II
), let
a
be as above, and assume that our o-minimal theory
T
defines a logarithm on a neighborhood of
a
.
Then
\delta a=\log(a)+c
for some
c\in\ker(\delta)\setminus\dcl_{\mathcal{L}}(\emptyset)
, and so
q\coloneqq\tp_{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}}(a|c)
is a
nonalgebraic
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\Delta}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
-forking extension of
p
.

Remark 5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{\ring}
and let
T=\text{ACF}_{0}
.
Consider the theory DCF
0,m
A, which is the model companion of the theory of fields with
m
commuting derivations
\Delta=(\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{m})
and a differential field automorphism
\sigma
(so
\sigma
is a field automorphism that also commutes with each
\delta_{i}
).
This theory was studied by León Sánchez in his thesis
[
34
]
; see also
[
35
]
.
There, it is shown that DCF
0,m
A is supersimple, with forking independence given by

\textstyle A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B\iff\Jet(A)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{\Jet(C)}\Jet(B)

where here,
\Jet(A)
is the closure of
A
under each
\delta_{i}
, as well as
\sigma
and
\sigma^{-1}
.
Let
\Jet^{+}(A)
denote the closure of
A
under each
\delta_{i}
and
\sigma
(but not necessarily
\sigma^{-1}
).
Then given any algebraic relation between elements of
\Jet(A)
over
\Jet(B)
, we can apply
\sigma
to this relation a sufficient number of times to obtain a relation between elements of
\Jet^{+}(A)
over
\Jet(B)
.
That is, we have

\textstyle A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{f}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B\iff\Jet^{+}(A)\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{\Jet(C)}\Jet(B).

Now
[
17
, Corollary A]
gives for any type
p=\tp({\bar{a}}|A)
in DCF
0,m
A a numerical polynomial
\omega_{p}
such that

\omega_{p}(t)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet_{\leqslant t}({\bar{a}})|\Jet(A))\quad\text{for $t\gg 0$},

where
\Jet_{\leqslant t}({\bar{a}})=\{\delta^{{\bar{u}}}\sigma^{r}({\bar{a}}):({\bar{u}},r)\in\mathbb{N}^{m+1},\ |{\bar{u}}|+r\leqslant t\}
.
Then the proofs of Proposition
4.6
, Corollary
4.7
and Theorem
4.9
go through in this setting to give
SU(p)\leqslant\rk_{\mathcal{P}}(\omega_{p})
; in particular,
SU(\text{DCF${}_{0,m}$A})=\omega^{m+1}
.
These bounds are implicit in
[
34
, Lemma 5.3.5]
, but not explicitly stated.
In the case
m=1
, something similar is done by Bustamante Medina to show that DCFA
\coloneqq
DCF
0,1
A has SU-rank
\omega^{2}

[
6
]
.

Appendix A
Extending independence to
\mathbb{M}^{\eq}

Let
T
be a pregeometric theory.
Then
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}
continues to satisfy exchange for real points over imaginary parameters
[
23
, Lemma 3.1]
, so
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}
extends to a well-defined rank
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
for any real tuple
{\bar{a}}
and any set of parameters
B
, possibly imaginary.

Given sets
A,B\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
, we call a real (possibly infinite) tuple
{\bar{b}}
a
basis
for
A
over
B
if
{\bar{b}}
is
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}
-independent over
B
and
A\subseteq\acl_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B{\bar{b}})
.
Note that two bases need not be the same size, but if
A
is finite, then it has a finite basis.
Following Gagelman, we further extend
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}
to finite imaginary tuples
{\bar{a}}
by setting

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)\coloneqq|{\bar{b}}|-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B{\bar{a}}),

where
{\bar{b}}
is a finite basis for
{\bar{a}}
over
B
.

Fact A.1

(
[
23
, Lemma 3.3 and Proposition 3.4]
)
.

Suppose
T
is pregeometric and let
{\bar{a}}
and
B
be as above.

(1)

For any finite real tuple
{\bar{b}}
with
{\bar{a}}\in\acl_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B{\bar{b}})
, we have

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B)-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B{\bar{a}}).

In particular,
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
does not depend on choice of basis.

(2)

There is a finite subset
B_{0}\subseteq B
with
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B_{0})=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
.

(3)

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}{\bar{b}}|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B{\bar{b}})+\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B)
for any finite imaginary tuple
{\bar{b}}
.

(4)

If
A\subseteq B
, then
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|A)\geqslant\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
.

(5)

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
only depends on
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)
.

Following Gagelman
[
23
]
, we say that
T
is
surgical
if it is pregeometric and if for every definable equivalence relation
E
on a definable set
X
, only finitely many
E
-classes have the same
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}
-dimension as
X
.

Fact A.2

(
[
23
, Proposition 3.5]
)
.

Let
T
be pregeometric.
Then
T
is surgical if and only if for all
a\in\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
and all
B\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
, we have
a\in\acl_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B)\iff\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(a|B)=0
.

When
T
is pregeometric, we use
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}
to define an independence relation
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
on small imaginary sets
A,B,C\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
as follows:

\textstyle A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B\iff\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|BC)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|C)\text{ for every finite subset }A_{0}\subseteq A.

As
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}
coincides with
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}
for real elements, we see that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
coincides with the independence relation
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\operatorname{M}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
defined in Subsection
1.2
for real sets.

Proposition A.3
.

Suppose
T
is pregeometric.
Then
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
is a real-strict independence relation on
\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
, the
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
-rank of
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)
coincides with
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
for all finite tuples
{\bar{a}}
, and
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
is strict if and only if
T
is surgical.

Proof.

Finite character follows from the definition of
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
, and invariance, monotonicity, base monotonicity, and normality follow from Fact
A.1
.

Local character:
First, suppose
A
is finite, and let
{\bar{b}}
be a finite basis for
A
.
Take a finite set
C\subseteq B
with
A\subseteq\acl_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(C{\bar{b}})
.
By increasing
C
, we may assume that
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|BA)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|CA)
.
Since
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|C)=|{\bar{b}}|
, we have
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A|C)
, so local character holds for
A
with
\kappa(A)=\aleph_{0}
.
Now suppose that
A
is infinite.
Let
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
enumerate the finite subsets of
A
, and for each
i
, use the finite case to take a finite subset
C_{i}\subseteq B
such that
A_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C_{i}}B
.
Set
C\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in I}C_{i}
, so
A_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
for each
i
by base monotonicity.
Finite character gives that
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
, so local character holds for
A
with
\kappa(A)=|A|
.

Full existence:
Let
A,B,C
be given and let
{\bar{b}}
be a basis for
A
over
C
.
We extend
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{b}}|C)
to a partial type over
B
by adding the
\mathcal{L}(B)
-formula
\neg\varphi({\bar{x}})
whenever
\varphi({\bar{x}})
is an
\mathcal{L}(B)
-formula asserting an algebraic relation between the coordinates of the variable
{\bar{x}}
.
Let
{\bar{b}}^{*}
be any tuple realizing this partial type, so
{\bar{b}}^{*}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
.
Let
\sigma
be an
\mathcal{L}(C)
-automorphism of
\mathbb{M}
mapping
{\bar{b}}
to
{\bar{b}}^{*}
, and set
A^{*}\coloneqq\sigma(A)
, so
A^{*}\equiv_{\mathcal{L}(C)}A
.
Let
A_{0}\subseteq A^{*}
be finite, and take a finite basis
{\bar{b}}_{0}\subseteq{\bar{b}}^{*}
for
A_{0}
over
C
.
We have

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|B)=|{\bar{b}}_{0}|-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}_{0}|BA_{0})\geqslant|{\bar{b}}_{0}|-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}_{0}|CA_{0})=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|C)\geqslant\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|B),

where the last inequality uses Fact
A.1
(
4
).
We conclude that
A^{*}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
by finite character.

Symmetry:
Suppose
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
.
We need to show that
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B_{0}|AC)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B_{0}|C)
for a given finite
B_{0}\subseteq B
.
Using Fact
A.1
(
2
), take a finite subset
A_{0}\subseteq A
with
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B_{0}|A_{0}C)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B_{0}|AC)
.
Take a finite basis
{\bar{b}}
for
A_{0}\cup B_{0}
over
C
.
By assumption,

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B_{0}C)-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|A_{0}B_{0}C)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|B_{0}C)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|C)=|{\bar{b}}|-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|A_{0}C).

Thus,

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B_{0}|AC)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B_{0}|A_{0}C)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|A_{0}C)-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|A_{0}B_{0}C)=|{\bar{b}}|-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B_{0}C)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B_{0}|C),

and we conclude that
B\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}A
by finite character.

Transitivity:
By symmetry, it suffices to show for
D\subseteq C\subseteq B
that if
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
and
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{D}C
, then
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{D}B
.
Let
A_{0}\subseteq A
be finite; then
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|D)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|C)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(A_{0}|B)
, as needed.

Strictness and real-strictness:

Suppose that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{B}a
, so
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(a|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(a|Ba)=0
.
When
a
is real, we have
a\in\acl_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(B)
, and we can also conclude this for imaginary
a
if and only if
T
is surgical by Fact
A.2
.

Rank equality:

Let
{\bar{a}}
be a tuple and
B
be a parameter set, possibly both imaginary, and let
p\coloneqq\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)
.
A straightforward induction on the
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\ \,
-rank of
p
shows that this rank is at most
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
.
To see that the
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\ \,
-rank of
p
is at least
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
, fix a finite basis
{\bar{b}}=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})
for
{\bar{a}}
over
B
.
Let
m\coloneqq\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
, so
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B{\bar{a}})=n-m
.
After rearranging, we may assume that
(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n-m})
are
\acl_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}
-independent over
B{\bar{a}}
.
For
i\leqslant m
, we set
{\bar{b}}_{i}\coloneqq(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n-m+i})
, and we calculate that

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B{\bar{b}}_{i})=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B{\bar{b}}_{i})-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B{\bar{a}}{\bar{b}}_{i})=m-i.

Thus,
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B{\bar{b}}_{i})
is an
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\ \,
-forking extension of
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B{\bar{b}}_{i-1})
for
0<i\leqslant m
.
In particular,
p
has
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\ \,
-rank at least
m
.
∎

Proposition A.4
.

The following are equivalent:

(1)

T
is surgical.

(2)

T
is rosy and pregeometric, and
