\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}=\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
.

(3)

T
is superrosy of þ-rank 1.

Proof.

(1)
\implies
(2):

Suppose
T
is surgical.
By Proposition
A.3
and Fact
1.4
,
T
is rosy and
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}
is coarser than
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\
.
To establish equality, we first claim that
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{a}}|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
for any tuple
{\bar{a}}
and small set
B
in
\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
.
Note that
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{a}}|B)\leqslant\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
by Fact
1.10
and Proposition
A.3
.
As þ-independence is strict, we have
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}(a|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(a|B)
for
a\in\mathbb{M}
, and it follows from the Lascar inequalities that
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{a}}|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
for
{\bar{a}}\in\mathbb{M}^{n}
.
Now let
{\bar{a}}
be an arbitrary finite tuple (possibly imaginary) and take a finite basis
{\bar{b}}
for
{\bar{a}}
over
B
.
Then using the Lascar inequalities again, we obtain

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|B)-\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{b}}|Ba)=U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{b}}|B)-U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{b}}|B{\bar{a}})=U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{a}}|B).

Now we fix
A,B,C\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
with
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
, and we need to show that
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}B
.
Take a finite tuple
{\bar{a}}
from
A
.
Then
{\bar{a}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}_{C}BC
by symmetry and normality, so

\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|BC)=U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{a}}|BC)=U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{a}}|C)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|C),

as desired.

(2)
\implies
(3):

Immediate, since
T
has
\mathop{\mathchoice{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\kern 4.53473pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 2.71246pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 4.53473pt}{\kern 3.95836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hbox to0.0pt{${}^{\eq}$}\hss}\lower 1.93747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 3.95836pt}}\ \,
-rank 1.

(3)
\implies
(1):
Our argument is quite similar to Gagelman’s proof of Fact
A.2
.
Suppose that
T
is superrosy of þ-rank 1.
It follows easily from the Lascar inequalities that
T
is pregeometric.
As þ-forking is strict and
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}(a|B)\leqslant 1
for
a\in\mathbb{M}
, we have
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}(a|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}(a|B)
for real singletons
a
.
Using the Lascar inequalities again, we see that
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{a}}|B)=\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}|B)
for all real tuples
{\bar{a}}
.
Now, suppose towards contradiction that we have an
\mathcal{L}(B)
-definable set
X
of dimension
d
and an
\mathcal{L}(B)
-definable equivalence relation
E
on
X
such that infinitely many
E
-classes have dimension
d
.
For
{\bar{a}}\in X
, we let
[{\bar{a}}]_{E}\in\mathbb{M}^{\eq}
denote the equivalence class of
{\bar{a}}
.
Take an infinite sequence
({\bar{a}}_{i})_{i<\omega}
in distinct
E
-classes with
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}_{i}|B[{\bar{a}}_{i}]_{E})=d
.
By saturation, we may arrange that all
{\bar{a}}_{i}
have the same
\mathcal{L}(B)
-type.
Let
e\coloneqq[{\bar{a}}_{0}]_{E}
, so
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}(e|B)
is non-algebraic and thus
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}(e|B)>0
.
The Lascar inequalities give
U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{a}}_{0}|B)\geqslant U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}(e|B)+U^{\textnormal{\T1\thh}}({\bar{a}}_{0}|Be)>d
, so
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}^{\eq}({\bar{a}}_{0}|B)>d
, a contradiction.
∎

Appendix B
The model completion of
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
for o-minimal
T

Let
T
be an o-minimal expansion of the theory of real closed ordered fields.
In this appendix, we show that
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
has a model completion, denoted
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
.
We then investigate some properties of
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
.

We maintain the same assumptions on
T
from Section
3
; namely, that
\mathcal{L}
contains function symbols for all
\mathcal{L}(\emptyset)
-definable functions, so that
T
has quantifier elimination and a universal axiomatization.
Given models
K\preccurlyeq M\models T
and
A\subseteq M
, we let
K\langle A\rangle
denote the substructure of
M
generated by
K
and
A
.
Then
K\langle A\rangle
is a model of
T
as well.
Given also an
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable set
X\subseteq K^{n}
, we write
X(M)
for the definable subset of
M^{n}
defined by the same formula as
X
.

Let
(K;\Delta)\models T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
.
We need the following fundamental lemma describing how
T
-derivations interact with
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable functions:

Fact B.1

(
[
16
, Lemma 2.12]
)
.

Let
\delta\in\Delta
, let
k>0
, and let
f
be an
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable
\mathcal{C}^{k}
-function on an open set
U\subseteq K^{n}
.
Then there is a unique
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable
\mathcal{C}^{k-1}
-function
f^{[\delta]}\colon U\to K
such that for any
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
-extension
(M;\Delta)\supseteq(K;\Delta)
and any
{\bar{u}}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})\in U(M)
, we have

\textstyle\delta f({\bar{u}})=f^{[\delta]}({\bar{u}})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}({\bar{u}})\delta u_{i}.

Let
\Gamma
be the free monoid generated by
\Delta=(\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{m})
.
For
\Lambda\subseteq\Gamma
, we let
K^{\Lambda}
consist of tuples
(z_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in\Lambda}
indexed by
\Lambda
.
When we say that
X\subseteq K^{\Lambda}
is definable, we mean that there is a finite
\Lambda_{0}\subseteq\Lambda
and a definable
X_{0}\subseteq K^{\Lambda_{0}}
such that
X=X_{0}\times K^{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{0}}
.

We view each
\gamma\in\Gamma
as an operator
K\to K
, and given a subset
\Gamma_{0}\subseteq\Gamma
and a tuple
{\bar{a}}\in K^{n}
, we let

\Gamma_{0}({\bar{a}})\coloneqq(\gamma a_{i})_{(i,\gamma)\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\times\Gamma_{0}}\in K^{n\times\Gamma_{0}}.

For
\gamma=\delta_{i_{1}}\delta_{i_{2}}\cdots\delta_{i_{r}}\in\Gamma
, we let
|\gamma|\coloneqq r
be the
order
of
\gamma
.
We partially order
\Gamma
by

\gamma_{1}\preccurlyeq\gamma_{2}\iff\gamma_{2}=\alpha\gamma_{1}\text{ for some }\alpha\in\Gamma.

Given a
\preccurlyeq
-downward closed subset
\Lambda\subseteq\Gamma
, we let
\Lambda^{\max}
be the set of
\preccurlyeq
-maximal elements of
\Lambda
, and we let
\Lambda^{\downarrow}\coloneqq\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\max}
.

Following
[
18
]
, we consider the following axiom schemes:

Deep:

For every finite
\preccurlyeq
-downward closed set
\Lambda\subseteq\Gamma
and every
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable set
A\subseteq K^{\Lambda}
, if the projection
\pi_{\Lambda^{\downarrow}}(A)\subseteq K^{\Lambda^{\downarrow}}
is open, then there is
a\in K
with
\Lambda(a)\in A
.

Wide:

For every
n
and every
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable set
X\subseteq K^{n}\times K^{n\times\Delta}
, if the projection
\pi_{n}(X)\subseteq K^{n}
is open, then there is
{\bar{a}}\in K^{n}
with
({\bar{a}},\Delta({\bar{a}}))\in X
.

Lemma B.2
.

(K;\Delta)
has a
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
-extension
(M;\Delta)
that satisfies the
Wide
axiom scheme.
Any such extension also satisfies the
Deep
axiom scheme.

Proof.

Let
X\subseteq K^{n}\times K^{n\times\Delta}
be an
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable set with
\pi_{n}(X)\subseteq K^{n}
open.
Take a
\dcl_{\mathcal{L}}(K)
-independent tuple
{\bar{a}}
in a
T
-extension of
K
with
{\bar{a}}\in\pi_{n}X(K\langle{\bar{a}}\rangle)
.
Using
[
16
, Lemma 2.13]
, we define a tuple of
T
-derivations
\Delta
on
K\langle{\bar{a}}\rangle
that extends those on
K
and satisfies
({\bar{a}},\Delta({\bar{a}}))\in X(K\langle{\bar{a}}\rangle)
.

One deduces the
Deep
axiom scheme from the
Wide
axiom scheme via the standard method of rewriting a higher-order differential equation as a system of first-order differential equations; see
[
18
, Lemma 4.6]
for details.
∎

Remark B.3
.

Following the more delicate construction in
[
16
, Lemma 4.2 and Proposition 4.3]
, we can define
T
-derivations that satisfy the
Wide
axioms on any
T
-extension
M
of
K
with
\rk_{\mathcal{L}}(M|K)=|M|\geqslant|T|
.

Let
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
extend
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
by the axiom scheme
Deep
given above.

Theorem B.4
.

T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
is the model completion of
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
.
The
Wide
axiom scheme is an alternative axiomatization of
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
over
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
B.2
, it suffices to show that the following diagram can be completed whenever
(K;\Delta)\subseteq(M;\Delta)
are models of
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
and
(\mathbb{M};\Delta)\models T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
is an
|M|^{+}
-saturated extension of
(K;\Delta)
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M;\Delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{M};\Delta)\models T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K;\Delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists}

Let
a\in M\setminus K
.
We need to find
b\in\mathbb{M}
with
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet(b)|K)=\tp_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet(a)|K)
, for then we can extend our inclusion
(K;\Delta)\subseteq(\mathbb{M};\Delta)
to an embedding
(K\langle\Jet(a)\rangle;\Delta)\to(\mathbb{M};\Delta)
and conclude by Zorn’s lemma.
By saturation, it suffices to show that for every finite
\Gamma_{0}\subseteq\Gamma
and every
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable set
X\subseteq K^{\Gamma_{0}}
with
\Gamma_{0}(a)\in X(M)
, there is
b\in\mathbb{M}
with
\Gamma_{0}(b)\in X(\mathbb{M})
.
One can show this following the proof of
[
18
, Lemma 4.8]
, using obvious modifications based on
[
16
]
, but for completeness, we give the details below.

Put a total ordering
\leqslant
on
\Gamma
, where

\delta_{i_{1}}\cdots\delta_{i_{k}}\leqslant\delta_{j_{1}}\cdots\delta_{j_{n}}\iff\text{either $k<n$, or $k=n$ and $(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})\leqslant_{lex}(j_{1},\ldots,j_{n})$}.

Then
\leqslant
refines
\preccurlyeq
, is left-invariant, and has order-type
\omega
.
Let

\Gamma_{a}\coloneqq\big\{\gamma\in\Gamma:\gamma(a)\not\in K\langle\Gamma^{<\gamma}(a)\rangle\big\}.

Then
\Gamma_{a}
is
\preccurlyeq
-downward closed by Fact
B.1
.

Now, let
\Gamma_{0}\subseteq\Gamma
be finite and let
X\subseteq K^{\Gamma_{0}}
be
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable with
\Gamma_{0}(a)\in X(M)
.
We may assume that
\Gamma_{0}
is
\leqslant
-downward closed.
Let
\Lambda_{0}\coloneqq\Gamma_{a}\cap\Gamma_{0}
.
Then
\Lambda_{0}(a)
is
\dcl_{\mathcal{L}}
-independent over
K
, so there is an
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable open neighborhood
U\subseteq\pi_{\Lambda_{0}}(X)
containing
\Lambda_{0}(a)
.
Let
\Lambda_{1}
be the set of
\preccurlyeq
-minimal elements of
\Gamma_{0}\setminus\Gamma_{a}
.
Then for each
\gamma\in\Lambda_{1}
, we have an
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable function
f_{\gamma}\colon M^{\Lambda_{0}}\to M
with
\gamma(a)=f_{\gamma}(\Lambda_{0}(a))
.
Let
\Lambda\coloneqq\Lambda_{0}\cup\Lambda_{1}
, let
x_{\Lambda}=(x_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in\Lambda}
be a tuple of variables, and consider the
\mathcal{L}(K)
-definable set

Y\coloneqq\{x_{\Lambda}\in K^{\Lambda}:x_{\Lambda_{0}}\in U\text{ and }x_{\gamma}=f_{\gamma}(x_{\Lambda_{0}})\}.

Then
\Lambda(a)\in Y
, so applying Fact
B.1
and shrinking
U
, we can arrange that for any
y
in any
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
-extension
(L;\Delta)
of
(K;\Delta)
, if
\Lambda(y)\in Y(L)
, then
\Gamma_{0}(y)\in X(L)
.
Thus, we need only find
b\in\mathbb{M}
with
\Lambda(b)\in Y(\mathbb{M})
.
Since
\Lambda^{\downarrow}\subseteq\Lambda_{0}
, the projection
\pi_{\Lambda^{\downarrow}}(Y)
is open, so we find our desired
b\in\mathbb{M}
using the
Deep
axioms.

By Lemma
B.2
, the
Wide
axioms imply the
Deep
axioms over
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
, and any model
(K;\Delta)\models T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
has a
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
-extension
(M;\Delta)
that satisfies the
Wide
axioms.
As
(K;\Delta)
is existentially closed in
(M;\Delta)
by the first part of the theorem,
(K;\Delta)
also satisfies the
Wide
axioms.
∎

For the remainder of this appendix, let
(\mathbb{M};\Delta)\models T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
be a monster model and let
B\subseteq\mathbb{M}
be a small subset with
B=\Jet(B)
.
From the proof of Theorem
B.4
, we see that for any
{\bar{a}}\in\mathbb{M}^{n}
, the type
\tp_{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}}({\bar{a}}|B)
is determined by
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet({\bar{a}})|B)
.
This yields a description of definable sets:

Corollary B.5
.

For any
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}(B)
-definable subset
X\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
, there is a finite
\Gamma_{0}\subseteq\Gamma
and an
\mathcal{L}(B)
-definable
\tilde{X}\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n\times\Gamma_{0}}
with

X=\{{\bar{a}}\in\mathbb{M}^{n}:\Gamma_{0}({\bar{a}})\in\tilde{X}\}.

Let
\ker(\Delta)\coloneqq\bigcap_{\delta\in\Delta}\ker(\delta)
be the
field of absolute constants
.
The previous corollary allows us to describe the induced structure on
\ker(\Delta)
.

Corollary B.6
.

Every
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}(B)
-definable subset of
\ker(\Delta)^{n}
is of the form
\ker(\Delta)^{n}\cap A
for some
\mathcal{L}(B)
-definable subset
A\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
.

Proof.

Exactly as in the proof of
[
16
, Lemma 4.23]
, using Corollary
B.5
.
∎

We can also lift combinatorial tameness from
T
to
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
:

Corollary B.7
.

The theory
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
is distal (in particular,
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
is NIP).

Proof.

Exactly as in the proof of
[
16
, Theorem 4.15]
, using Corollary
B.5
.
∎

Theorem B.8

(O-minimal open core)
.

Any
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}(B)
-definable open subset of
\mathbb{M}^{n}
is
\mathcal{L}(B)
-definable.

Proof.

We apply the characterization in
[
5
, Corollary 3.1]
.
For a fixed
n
, we need to find
D\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
such that:

(1)

D
is dense in
\mathbb{M}^{n}
,

(2)

for every
{\bar{a}}\in D
and open
U\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
, if
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)
is realized in
U
, then it is realized in
U\cap D
, and

(3)

for every
{\bar{a}}\in D
, the type
\tp_{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}}({\bar{a}}|B)
is implied by
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)
in conjunction with “
{\bar{a}}\in D
”.

We claim that
D\coloneqq\ker(\Delta)^{n}
realizes these three conditions.
For any open
\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{M})
-definable
U\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
, the
Wide
axioms give
{\bar{a}}\in U
with
\Delta({\bar{a}})={\bar{0}}
, so condition (1) holds.
Condition (3) follows immediately from Corollary
B.6
, so it remains to establish condition (2).
Let
{\bar{a}}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\in\ker(\Delta)^{n}
be given and let
U\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{n}
be an open set containing a realization of
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)
.
Let
k\leqslant n
and suppose without loss of generality that
{\bar{a}}_{0}\coloneqq(a_{1},\ldots,a_{k})
is a maximal
\dcl_{\mathcal{L}}(B)
-independent subtuple of
{\bar{a}}
.
Take an
\mathcal{L}(B)
-definable map
f=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})\colon\mathbb{M}^{k}\to\mathbb{M}^{n}
with
f({\bar{a}}_{0})={\bar{a}}
.
Since
f
is generically continuous and
U
contains a realization of
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)
, we find an open set
V\subseteq\mathbb{M}^{k}
containing a realization of
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}_{0}|B)
with
f(V)\subseteq U
.
Since the set of realizations of
\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}_{0}|B)
is open and
\ker(\Delta)^{k}
is dense in
\mathbb{M}^{k}
, we find
{\bar{c}}_{0}\in\ker(\Delta)^{k}\cap V
with
{\bar{c}}_{0}\models\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}_{0}|B)
.
Set
{\bar{c}}\coloneqq f({\bar{c}}_{0})
, so
{\bar{c}}\in U
and
{\bar{c}}\models\tp_{\mathcal{L}}({\bar{a}}|B)
.
We need to show that
{\bar{c}}\in\ker(\Delta)^{n}
.
For
\delta\in\Delta
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, Fact
B.1
gives

0=\delta a_{j}=\delta f_{j}({\bar{a}}_{0})=f_{j}^{[\delta]}({\bar{a}}_{0})+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{\partial f_{j}}{\partial x_{i}}({\bar{a}}_{0})\delta a_{i}=f_{j}^{[\delta]}({\bar{a}}_{0}).

It follows that
f_{j}^{[\delta]}({\bar{c}}_{0})=0
as well, so
\delta c_{j}=\delta f_{j}({\bar{c}}_{0})=0
, since
{\bar{c}}_{0}\in\ker(\Delta)^{k}
.
As this holds for all
\delta
and all
j
, we have
{\bar{c}}\in\ker(\Delta)^{n}
as desired.
∎

Using Theorem
B.8
and following the proofs of
[
16
, Corollary 5.14 and Theorem 5.19]
, we obtain a characterization of
\dcl_{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}}
and elimination of imaginaries:

Corollary B.9
.

For any
A\subseteq\mathbb{M}
, we have
\dcl_{\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}}(A)=\dcl_{\mathcal{L}}(\Jet(A))
.
In particular, the
\mathcal{L}^{\Delta}
-definably closed sets are exactly the models of
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}
.

Corollary B.10
.

The theory
T^{\Delta,\operatorname{nc}}_{g}
eliminates imaginaries.
